The proofs are located in Sect.
6.4
.
Before proving the theorems,
we will first establish,
in separate sections, some of their key aspects.
We start by stating our main theorem for solutions without symmetry.

Theorem 6.1

(Precise version of stable Big Bang formation without symmetry assumptions)
.

Let
\widetilde{\bf g}:=-dt\otimes dt+\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{2\widetilde{q}_{I}}dx^{I}\otimes dx^{I}
,

\widetilde{\psi}:=\widetilde{B}\log t
be an explicit generalized Kasner solution on

(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
,
where the constants
\{\widetilde{q}_{I}\}_{I=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
and
\widetilde{B}

satisfy the algebraic constraints

\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\widetilde{q}_{I}=1
and
\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\widetilde{q}_{I}^{2}=1-\widetilde{B}^{2}

as well as the following
stability condition
:

\displaystyle\mathop{\max_{I,J,B=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}}_{I<J}\{\widetilde{q}_{I}+\widetilde{q}_{J}-\widetilde{q}_{B}\}<1.

(6.1)

Note that in the vacuum case, we have
\widetilde{B}=0
.
As we discussed in Sect.
1.5
, in the vacuum case,
the set of Kasner solutions satisfying the algebraic constraints
and the condition (
6.1
) is non-empty
when
\mathfrak{D}\geq 10
, while in the presence of a scalar field,
the set of Kasner solutions satisfying the algebraic constraints
and the condition (
6.1
) is non-empty
when
\mathfrak{D}\geq 3
.
Let
\widetilde{k}_{IJ}=-\widetilde{q}_{\underline{I}}\updelta_{\underline{I}J}t^{-1}

be the components of the second fundamental form of
\Sigma_{t}
relative to the Kasner metric,
with respect to the background orthonormal frame vectors

\widetilde{e}_{I}=t^{-\widetilde{q}_{\underline{I}}}\partial_{\underline{I}}
,
where we recall that we do not sum repeated underlined indices.
Let
(\Sigma_{1}=\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}},\mathring{g},\mathring{k},\mathring{\psi},\mathring{\phi})

be geometric initial data (see Sect.
1.1
)
for the Einstein-scalar field equations verifying the constraint equations (
1.2a
)–(
1.2b
) and the CMC condition
\mathrm{tr}k=-1
(see Remark
1.5
),
and let
\{\mathring{e}_{I}\}_{I=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

be the initial orthonormal frame (on
\Sigma_{1}
)
constructed in Sect.
5.11
.
Note that
\mathring{\psi}=\mathring{\phi}=0

corresponds to the Einstein-vacuum equations.
Let:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathring{\upalpha}:=&\sum_{i,j=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\|\mathring{g}_{ij}-\updelta_{ij}\|_{H^{N+1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}+\sum_{i,j=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\|\mathring{k}_{ij}+\widetilde{q}_{\underline{i}}\updelta_{\underline{i}j}\|_{H^{N}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\\
&\ \ +\|\mathring{\psi}\|_{H^{N+1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}+\|\mathring{\phi}-\widetilde{B}\|_{H^{N}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\end{split}

(6.2)

denote the norm of the perturbation of the geometric initial data away from the Kasner data,
as defined in (
5.61
).
Assume that:

•

N_{0}\geq 1
is a fixed positive integer (we are free to choose
N_{0}
).

•

A_{*}
is sufficiently large in a manner that depends on
\mathfrak{D}
and the parameters

q
and
\upsigma
fixed in (
3.4
).

•

N
is sufficiently large in a manner that depends on
N_{0},A_{*},\mathfrak{D},q,
and
\upsigma
.

•

\mathring{\upalpha}
is sufficiently small in a manner that depends on

N,N_{0},A_{*},\mathfrak{D},q,
and
\upsigma
.

Then the following conclusions hold.

Existence and norm estimates on
(0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
.

The initial data launch a unique
solution

(n,k_{IJ},\upgamma_{IJB},e_{I}^{i},\upomega_{i}^{I},\psi)_{I,J,B,i=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

to the reduced Einstein-scalar field equations of Proposition
2.2

existing on the slab
(t,x)\in(0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
.
Moreover, if we define
g_{ij}
and
{\bf{g}}
in terms of the reduced variables by

g_{ij}:=\upomega_{i}^{A}\upomega_{j}^{A}

and

{\bf g}:=-n^{2}dt\otimes dt+g_{ab}dx^{a}\otimes dx^{b}

(where
t
is the CMC time function and

\{x^{i}\}_{i=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
are the transported spatial coordinates),
then the tensorfields
({\bf g},\psi)

are also classical solutions to the Einstein-scalar field system (
1.1a
)–(
1.1b
)
on
(0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
.
In addition, there exists a constant
C=C_{N,N_{0},A_{*},\mathfrak{D},q,\upsigma}

such that the following estimates hold for
t\in(0,1]
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{I,i=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{q}\|e_{I}^{i}-\widetilde{e}_{I}^{i}\|_{W^{N_{0},\infty}(\Sigma_{t})}+\sum_{I,i=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{q}\|\upomega_{i}^{I}-\widetilde{\upomega}_{i}^{I}\|_{W^{N_{0},\infty}(\Sigma_{t})}\\
&\ \ +\sum_{I,J,B=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{q}\|\upgamma_{IJB}\|_{W^{N_{0},\infty}(\Sigma_{t})}+\sum_{I,J=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t\|k_{IJ}-\widetilde{k}_{IJ}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\Sigma_{t})}\\
&\ \ +\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{q}\|e_{I}\psi\|_{W^{N_{0},\infty}(\Sigma_{t})}+\|t\partial_{t}\psi-\widetilde{B}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\Sigma_{t})}\\
&\ \ +t^{-\upsigma}\|n-1\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\Sigma_{t})}+\sum_{I}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{q-\upsigma}\|e_{I}n\|_{W^{N_{0},\infty}(\Sigma_{t})}\\
&\leq C\mathring{\upalpha},\end{split}

(6.3a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{I,i=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{A_{*}+q}\|e_{I}^{i}-\widetilde{e}_{I}^{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{N}(\Sigma_{t})}+\sum_{I,i=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{A_{*}+q}\|\upomega_{i}^{I}-\widetilde{\upomega}_{i}^{I}\|_{\dot{H}^{N}(\Sigma_{t})}\\
&\ \ +\sum_{I,J,B=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{A_{*}+1}\|\upgamma_{IJB}\|_{\dot{H}^{N}(\Sigma_{t})}+\sum_{I,J=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{A_{*}+1}\|k_{IJ}\|_{\dot{H}^{N}(\Sigma_{t})}\\
&\ \ +\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}t^{A_{*}+1}\|e_{I}\psi\|_{\dot{H}^{N}(\Sigma_{t})}+t^{A_{*}+1}\|\partial_{t}\psi\|_{\dot{H}^{N}(\Sigma_{t})}\\
&\ \ +t^{A_{*}}\|n\|_{\dot{H}^{N}(\Sigma_{t})}+t^{A_{*}+1}\|\vec{e}n\|_{\dot{H}^{N}(\Sigma_{t})}\\
&\leq C\mathring{\upalpha}.\end{split}

(6.3b)

Kasner-like behavior.

The scalar component functions
\left\{tk_{IJ}(t,x)\right\}_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
of the
normalized second fundamental form of
\Sigma_{t}
with respect to the
g
-orthonormal
frame
\{e_{I}(t,x)\}_{I=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
,
as well as the normalized time derivative
t\partial_{t}\psi(t,x)
of the scalar field,
have continuous
W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})
limits,
denoted respectively by
40
40

40

Here, we are slightly abusing notation by, for example, using the
expression
\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(x)
to denote the function
x\rightarrow\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(x)
.

\left\{\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(x)\right\}_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

and
B^{(\infty)}(x)
, as
t\downarrow 0
.
Moreover, the following estimates hold for
t\in(0,1]
:

\displaystyle\sum_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\|tk_{IJ}(t,\cdot)-\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathring{\upalpha}t^{\upsigma},

\displaystyle\|t\partial_{t}\psi(t,\cdot)-B^{(\infty)}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathring{\upalpha}t^{\upsigma},

(6.4a)

\displaystyle\sum_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}+\widetilde{q}_{\underline{I}}\updelta_{\underline{I}J}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathring{\upalpha},

\displaystyle\|B^{(\infty)}-\widetilde{B}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathring{\upalpha}.

(6.4b)

In addition, for each
x\in\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
,
the symmetric
\mathfrak{D}\times\mathfrak{D}
matrix

(-\upkappa^{(\infty)}_{IJ}(x))_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

has
\mathfrak{D}
(possibly repeated) eigenvalues

q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)

– which are the “final” Kasner exponents of the perturbed spacetime –
that can be ordered such that:

\displaystyle q_{1}^{(\infty)},\cdots,q_{\mathfrak{D}}^{(\infty)}\in C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}),

(6.5)

where
C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})
is the space of Lipschitz-continuous functions on
\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
.
Moreover, the following estimate holds, where

\|f\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}:=\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}+\sup_{x,y\in\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}},\,x\neq y}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{d(x,y)}
,
and
d(x,y)
is the Euclidean distance between
x
and
y
in
\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
:

\displaystyle\|q_{I}^{(\infty)}-\widetilde{q}_{I}\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\leq C\mathring{\upalpha}.

(6.6)

Moreover, the
\left\{q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)\right\}_{I=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
and
B^{(\infty)}(x)

satisfy the following pointwise algebraic relations:

\displaystyle\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\left[q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=1-\left[B^{(\infty)}(x)\right]^{2}.

(6.7)

Curvature-blowup.

The Kretschmann scalar of
{\bf g}
,
namely
{\bf Riem}^{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}{\bf Riem}_{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}
,
blows up as
t\downarrow 0
, as
is evident from the following pointwise estimate, valid
for
(t,x)\in(0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\bf Riem}^{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}{\bf Riem}_{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}(t,x)&=4t^{-4}\left\{\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\left[(q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x))^{2}-q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)\right]^{2}+\sum_{1\leq I<J\leq\mathfrak{D}}(q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x))^{2}(q_{J}^{(\infty)}(x))^{2}\right\}\\
&\ \ +\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upalpha}t^{-4+\upsigma})\\
&=4t^{-4}\left\{\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\left[\widetilde{q}_{I}^{2}-\widetilde{q}_{I}\right]^{2}+\sum_{1\leq I<J\leq\mathfrak{D}}\widetilde{q}_{I}^{2}\widetilde{q}_{J}^{2}\right\}+\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upalpha}t^{-4}).\end{split}

(6.8)

Inextendibility.

The spacetime is past-inextendible as a
C^{2}
Lorentzian manifold.

Remark 6.2

(No regular limit is claimed for the orthonormal frame vectorfields)
.

Despite the convergence of the
normalized component functions
\left\{tk_{IJ}(t,x)\right\}_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
,
our proof does not yield (or require!)
that the component functions
\{e_{I}^{i}(t,x)\}_{I,i=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

of the frame vectorfields with respect to the transported spatial coordinates
can be rescaled by powers of
t

so as to have non-trivial, regular limits as
t\downarrow 0
.

Remark 6.3

(Sharper asymptotics)
.

Although Theorems
6.1
and
6.6
yield the most interesting and salient
features of the stable blowup, by using the estimates provided by the theorems,
one could try to derive sharper asymptotics for the solution
by treating the evolution equations
as ODEs (with derivative-losing source terms), perhaps also employing
a different gauge for the already constructed singular solution.
In some symmetric regimes, gauges are known in which one can prove stability and sharp asymptotics
at the level of the metric components.
For example, in polarized
U(1)
-symmetry
[
1
]
,
41
41

41

The work
[
1
]
is concerned with
the near-Schwarzschild black hole interior problem, where the symmetry class is called “polarized axi-symmetry.”
This regime has some analytical commonalities with the polarized
U(1)
-symmetric solutions that we
treat in Theorem
6.6
; see the end of Sect.
1.8.1
.

the authors proved Big Bang formation and
derived sharp asymptotics for various solution variables by using
frames that are well-adapted to the different Kasner directions.
See also the recent works
[
4
,
5
]

on the Einstein-vacuum equations in three spatial dimensions,
in which the authors used an areal time foliation
to prove stability and sharper asymptotics at the level of the metric components
for a subset of the Kasner solutions
near their Big Bang singularities
under polarized
\mathbb{T}^{2}
-symmetric perturbations of the initial data.

Remark 6.4

(In general, no additional regularity is claimed for the final Kasner exponents)
.

Although
\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\in W^{N_{0}+1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})
(where we assume
N_{0}\geq 1
),
in general, the function space
C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})
in (
6.5
)
and the norm
\|\cdot\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

on LHS (
6.6
) are optimal
(e.g., they cannot be improved to
C^{m}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})
for any integer
m\geq 2
),
due to the fact that the background solution is allowed to have repeated Kasner exponents.
However, by analyzing the dependence of the characteristic polynomial of
the matrix
\left(tk_{IJ}(t,x)\right)_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
on the entries
tk_{IJ}
,
we could show that if
\widetilde{q}_{I}\neq\widetilde{q}_{J}

for
1\leq I<J\leq\mathfrak{D}
, then the space
C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})
in (
6.5
)
could be replaced with
C^{N_{0}+1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})

and
the norm
\|\cdot\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

on LHS (
6.6
)
could be replaced with
\|\cdot\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}
.
This would require an additional smallness assumption on

\mathring{\upalpha}
:
\mathring{\upalpha}\ll\underset{1\leq I<J\leq\mathfrak{D}}{\min}|\widetilde{q}_{I}-\widetilde{q}_{J}|
.
We thank one of the referees for providing helpful comments tied to this issue.

Remark 6.5
.

The eigen
vectors
of the symmetric matrix
(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(x))_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

might fail to be continuous in
x
, for example in the case
where the
q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)
’s have contact points of infinite order;
see
[
38
, Chapter 2, Example 5.3]
.

We now state our main theorem for polarized
U(1)
-symmetric solutions.

Theorem 6.6

(Precise version of stable Big Bang formation for
polarized
U(1)
-symmetric Einstein-vacuum solutions in
1+3
dimensions)
.

Let
\widetilde{\bf g}=-dt\otimes dt+t^{2\widetilde{q}_{1}}dx^{1}\otimes dx^{1}+t^{2\widetilde{q}_{2}}dx^{2}\otimes dx^{2}+t^{2\widetilde{q}_{3}}dx^{3}\otimes dx^{3}

be a “background” Kasner solution on
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{T}^{3}

with Kasner exponents satisfying:

\displaystyle\sum_{I=1}^{3}\widetilde{q}_{I}=\sum_{I=1}^{3}\widetilde{q}_{I}^{2}=1,\qquad\max_{I=1,2,3}\widetilde{q}_{I}<1.

(6.9)

Let
\widetilde{k}_{IJ}=-\widetilde{q}_{\underline{I}}\updelta_{\underline{I}J}t^{-1}

be the components of the second fundamental form of
\Sigma_{t}
relative to the Kasner metric,
with respect to the background orthonormal frame vectors

\widetilde{e}_{I}=t^{-\widetilde{q}_{\underline{I}}}\partial_{\underline{I}}
.
Let
(\Sigma_{1}=\mathbb{T}^{3},\mathring{g},\mathring{k})
be polarized
U(1)
-symmetric initial data
(see Sects.
1.1
and
1.5.2
) for the Einstein-vacuum equations
verifying the constraint equations (
1.2a
)–(
1.2b
)
(with
\mathring{\psi}=\mathring{\phi}\equiv 0
)
and the CMC condition
\mathrm{tr}\mathring{k}=-1
(see Remark
1.5
),
and such that
\mathring{X}:=\partial_{3}
is the hypersurface-orthogonal Killing vectorfield of the data.
Let
\mathring{\upalpha}
be the norm of the perturbation of the initial data away from the Kasner data,
as defined in (
5.61
)
(where the scalar field data on RHS (
5.61
) are vanishing by assumption).
Let
\{e_{I}\}_{I=1,2,3}
be the
g
-orthonormal frame
obtained by constructing the initial orthonormal frame as in
Sect.
5.11

and then using Lemma
2.4

to ensure that throughout the evolution,
the corresponding frame solution to the Fermi–Walker transport equation (
2.8
)
verifies
e_{3}=\frac{\partial_{3}}{\sqrt{g_{33}}}

and
\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{3}}e_{I}=0
for
I=1,2,3
, where
\mathcal{L}
denotes Lie differentiation.
Assume that the parameters
N
,
N_{0}
,
A_{*}
,
q
,
\upsigma
,
\mathring{\upalpha}
satisfy the assumptions of Theorem

6.1
, where in polarized
U(1)
-symmetry,
q,\upsigma
are fixed constants satisfying:

\displaystyle 0<2\upsigma<2\upsigma+\max\{|\widetilde{q}_{1}|,|\widetilde{q}_{2}|,|\widetilde{q}_{3}|\}<q<1-2\upsigma.

(6.10)

Then the conclusions stated in Theorem
6.1
hold for the
solution to the reduced equations of Proposition
2.2

(which also yields a solution to the Einstein-vacuum equations, i.e., (
1.1a
) with
\psi\equiv 0
)
that arises from the prescribed polarized
U(1)
-symmetric initial data
(\mathring{g},\mathring{k})
.
Moreover, the solution is polarized
U(1)
-symmetric
in the sense that relative to the transported spatial coordinates,

\partial_{3}
is a hypersurface-orthogonal Killing vectorfield of the spacetime metric
{\bf g}
,
and
{\bf g}
is of the form (
2.37
).

6.2
Limiting functions and Kasner-like behavior

In the next proposition, we show that the scalar functions

\left\{tk_{IJ}(t,\cdot)\right\}_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

and
t\partial_{t}\psi(t,\cdot)
have limits in
W^{N_{0}+1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,
as
t\downarrow 0
. Moreover, the limiting fields obey a limiting Hamiltonian constraint equation
and exhibit other “Kasner-like” properties.

Proposition 6.7

(Asymptotic, Kasner-like limits)
.

Under the assumptions and conclusions of Proposition
5.28
,
the scalar component functions
\left\{tk_{IJ}(t,x)\right\}_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

of the normalized second fundamental form of
\Sigma_{t}

with respect to the
g
-orthonormal
frame
\{e_{I}(t,x)\}_{I=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

and the normalized scalar field velocity

t\partial_{t}\psi(t,x)
have continuous limits in
W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})
,
denoted respectively by

\left\{\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(x)\right\}_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

and
B^{(\infty)}(x)
, as
t\downarrow 0
.
Moreover, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\sum_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\|tk_{IJ}(t,\cdot)-\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{\upsigma},

\displaystyle\|t\partial_{t}\psi(t,\cdot)-B^{(\infty)}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{\upsigma},

(6.11a)

\displaystyle\sum_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}+\widetilde{q}_{\underline{I}}\updelta_{\underline{I}J}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon},

\displaystyle\|B^{(\infty)}-\widetilde{B}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}.

(6.11b)

In addition, for each
x\in\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
,
the symmetric
\mathfrak{D}\times\mathfrak{D}
matrix

(-\upkappa^{(\infty)}_{IJ}(x))_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

has
\mathfrak{D}
(possibly repeated) eigenvalues

q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)
– which are the “final” Kasner exponents of the perturbed spacetime –
that can be ordered such that
q_{1}^{(\infty)},\cdots,q_{\mathfrak{D}}^{(\infty)}\in C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})

(see the discussion surrounding (
6.5
) for the definition of this function space and the norm)
and such that the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{I=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\|q_{I}^{(\infty)}-\widetilde{q}_{I}\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}.

(6.12)

Moreover, the
\left\{q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)\right\}_{I=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
and
B^{(\infty)}(x)

satisfy the following pointwise algebraic relations:

\displaystyle\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)=1,\qquad\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\left[q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)\right]^{2}=1-\left[B^{(\infty)}(x)\right]^{2}.

(6.13)

Proof.

Let
\{t_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset(0,1]
be a decreasing sequence of times
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}t_{n}=0
.
A straightforward modification of the proof of (
5.48
),
based on the evolution equation (
5.31a
)
and the estimate (
5.58
),
yields that when
0<a<b\leq 1
, we have

\|ak_{IJ}(a,\cdot)-bk_{IJ}(b,\cdot)\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}\int_{a}^{b}s^{-1+\upsigma}\mathbb{D}(s)\,ds\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}b^{\upsigma}
.
Hence,
\{t_{n}k_{IJ}(t_{n},\cdot)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is a Cauchy sequence
in
W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})
, and its limit, which we denote by
\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}
,
verifies

\|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}-tk_{IJ}(t,\cdot)\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{\upsigma}

for
t\in(0,1]
.
In particular,

\|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}-k_{IJ}(1,\cdot)\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}
.
Since

\|k_{IJ}(1,\cdot)+\widetilde{q}_{\underline{I}}\updelta_{\underline{I}J}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}=\|k_{IJ}(1,\cdot)-\widetilde{k}_{IJ}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}
,
we infer from the triangle inequality that

\|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}+\widetilde{q}_{\underline{I}}\updelta_{\underline{I}J}\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}
. We have therefore proved
(
6.11a
)
and
(
6.11b
) for
\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}
.
Moreover, the symmetric matrix
(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(x))_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}

is
\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon})
-close to the diagonal matrix

\mbox{diag}(-\widetilde{q}_{1},\cdots,-\widetilde{q}_{\mathfrak{D}})
.
Thus, at each fixed
x
,

-(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(x))_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
is diagonalizable,
and by standard perturbation theory
(see
[
63
, Equation (3.6) in Chapter IV]
),
its (possibly repeated) eigenvalues
q_{I}(x)
can be ordered such that

q_{1}^{(\infty)}(x),\cdots,q_{\mathfrak{D}}^{(\infty)}(x)\in C^{0,1}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})

and such that the following pointwise estimate holds for all
x,y\in\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{I=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\left|q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)-\widetilde{q}_{I}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\left|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(x)+\widetilde{q}_{\underline{I}}\updelta_{\underline{I}J}\right|,

(6.14)

\displaystyle\sum_{I=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\left|q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)-q_{I}^{(\infty)}(y)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}\left|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(x)-\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(y)\right|.

(6.15)

From (
6.14
)–(
6.15
),
the standard inequality

\left|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(x)-\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}(y)\right|\lesssim\|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}d(x,y)

(where
d(x,y)
is the Euclidean distance between
x
and
y
in
\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
),
and the first estimate in (
6.11b
),
we conclude (
6.12
).

The convergence results and estimates for
t\partial_{t}\psi

can be proved in a similar fashion by making
straightforward modifications to the proof of (
5.51
).

To derive the first equation in (
6.13
), we employ the CMC condition (
2.17
)
and the estimate

\|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}-tk_{IJ}(t,\cdot)\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{\upsigma}

proved above to deduce the following pointwise estimate:

\displaystyle-1=t\text{tr}k(t,x)=\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{\upsigma})+\text{tr}\upkappa^{(\infty)}(x)=\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{\upsigma})-\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x),

(6.16)

where to obtain the last equality, we used that the trace of the
\mathfrak{D}\times\mathfrak{D}

matrix
\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\right)_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
is the sum of its eigenvalues

-q_{1}^{(\infty)},\cdots,-q_{\mathfrak{D}}^{(\infty)}
. Taking the limit
t\downarrow 0

on RHS (
6.16
), we obtain the desired equation.

To derive the second equation in (
6.13
),
we multiply the Hamiltonian constraint (
2.26a
) by
t^{2}

and use Definition
3.1
,
the estimate (
5.58
),
the inequalities in (
3.4
),
and the estimates

\|\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}-tk_{IJ}(t,\cdot)\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{\upsigma}

and

\|B^{(\infty)}-t\partial_{t}\psi(t,\cdot)\|_{W^{N_{0}+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}})}\lesssim\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{\upsigma}

noted above to deduce the following pointwise estimate:

\displaystyle\begin{split}1&=t^{2}k_{CD}(t,x)k_{CD}(t,x)-t^{2}\left\{2e_{C}\upgamma_{DDC}-\upgamma_{CDE}\upgamma_{EDC}-\upgamma_{CCD}\upgamma_{EED}\right\}(t,x)\\
&\ \ +n^{-2}\left[t\partial_{t}\psi(t,x)\right]^{2}+t^{2}\left[e_{C}\psi(t,x)\right]e_{C}\psi(t,x)\\
&=\upkappa_{CD}^{(\infty)}(x)\upkappa_{CD}^{(\infty)}(x)+\left[B^{(\infty)}(x)\right]^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{\upsigma})=\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\left[q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)\right]^{2}+\left[B^{(\infty)}(x)\right]^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{\upsigma}),\end{split}

(6.17)

where to obtain the last equality, we used the fact that

\upkappa_{CD}^{(\infty)}(x)\upkappa_{CD}^{(\infty)}(x)

is equal to the sum of the squares of eigenvalues of the matrix
\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\right)_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
.
The desired second equation in (
6.13
)
now follows from taking the limit
t\downarrow 0
on RHS (
6.17
).
This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

6.3
Monotonic blowup of curvature

In the following proposition,
we show that the Kretschmann scalars of the solutions studied in the present paper blow up like
t^{-4}
.

Proposition 6.8

(Monotonic blow up of the Kretschmann scalar)
.

Under the assumptions and conclusions of Proposition
5.28
,
the Kretschmann scalar

{\bf Riem}^{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}{\bf Riem}_{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}

obeys the following pointwise estimate for
(t,x)\in(0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{D}}
,
where the functions
\left\{q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)\right\}_{I=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
are
as in the conclusions of Proposition
6.7
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\bf Riem}^{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}{\bf Riem}_{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}(t,x)&=4t^{-4}\left\{\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\left[(q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x))^{2}-q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x)\right]^{2}+\sum_{1\leq I<J\leq\mathfrak{D}}(q_{I}^{(\infty)}(x))^{2}(q_{J}^{(\infty)}(x))^{2}\right\}\\
&\ \ +\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{-4+\upsigma})\\
&=4t^{-4}\left\{\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\left[\widetilde{q}_{I}^{2}-\widetilde{q}_{I}\right]^{2}+\sum_{1\leq I<J\leq\mathfrak{D}}\widetilde{q}_{I}^{2}\widetilde{q}_{J}^{2}\right\}+\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{-4}).\end{split}

(6.18)

Proof.

We first use the standard symmetries and antisymmetries of the Riemann curvature
tensor of
{\bf{g}}
to compute the following identity for its Kretschmann scalar:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\bf Riem}^{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}{\bf Riem}_{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}&={\bf Riem}(e_{A},e_{I},e_{B},e_{J}){\bf Riem}(e_{A},e_{I},e_{B},e_{J})\\
&\ \ +4{\bf Riem}(e_{0},e_{I},e_{0},e_{J}){\bf Riem}(e_{0},e_{I},e_{0},e_{J})\\
&\ \ -4{\bf Riem}(e_{A},e_{I},e_{0},e_{J}){\bf Riem}(e_{A},e_{I},e_{0},e_{J}).\end{split}

(6.19)

Next, using Gauss’ equation (
2.29
),
(
2.9
),
(
2.19
),
Definition
3.1
,
the estimate (
5.58
),
the inequalities in (
3.4
),
and the convergence estimate (
6.11a
) for
tk_{IJ}
,
we derive the following pointwise estimate:

\displaystyle\begin{split}t^{2}{\bf Riem}(e_{A},e_{I},e_{B},e_{J})&=(tk_{IJ})(tk_{AB})-(tk_{AJ})(tk_{BI})+t^{2}e_{A}\upgamma_{IJB}-t^{2}e_{I}\upgamma_{AJB}\\
&\ \ -t^{2}\upgamma_{AIC}\upgamma_{CJB}-t^{2}\upgamma_{IJC}\upgamma_{ABC}+t^{2}\upgamma_{IAC}\upgamma_{CJB}+t^{2}\upgamma_{AJC}\upgamma_{IBC}\\
&=(tk_{IJ})(tk_{AB})-(tk_{AJ})(tk_{BI})+\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon})t^{\upsigma}=\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{AB}^{(\infty)}-\upkappa_{AJ}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{BI}^{(\infty)}+\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon})t^{\upsigma}.\end{split}

(6.20)

Similarly, with the help of the Codazzi equations (
2.33
) and (
2.19
),
we compute the following pointwise estimate:

\displaystyle\begin{split}t^{2}{\bf Riem}(e_{A},e_{I},e_{0},e_{J})&=t^{2}\left\{e_{A}^{c}\partial_{c}k_{IJ}-e_{I}^{c}\partial_{c}k_{AJ}-\upgamma_{AIB}k_{BJ}-\upgamma_{AJB}k_{IB}+\upgamma_{IAB}k_{BJ}+\upgamma_{IJB}k_{AB}\right\}\\
&=\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon})t^{\upsigma}.\end{split}

(6.21)

Similarly, with the help of (
2.28
) and the evolution equation (
2.22a
),
we deduce the following pointwise estimate:

\displaystyle\begin{split}t^{2}{\bf Riem}(e_{0},e_{I},e_{0},e_{J})&=-tk_{IJ}-(tk_{IC})(tk_{CJ})\\
&\ \ +t^{2}\left\{e_{D}^{c}\partial_{c}\upgamma_{IJD}-e_{I}^{c}\partial_{c}\upgamma_{DJD}-\upgamma_{DIC}\upgamma_{CJD}-\upgamma_{DDC}\upgamma_{IJC}-(e_{I}\psi)e_{J}\psi\right\}\\
&=-\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}-\upkappa_{IC}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{CJ}^{(\infty)}+\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon})t^{\upsigma}.\end{split}

(6.22)

Inserting (
6.20
)–(
6.22
) into (
6.19
),
we deduce the following pointwise estimate:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\bf Riem}^{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}{\bf Riem}_{\alpha\mu\beta\nu}\\
&=t^{-4}\Big\{\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{AB}^{(\infty)}-\upkappa_{AJ}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{BI}^{(\infty)}\right)\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{AB}^{(\infty)}-\upkappa_{AJ}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{BI}^{(\infty)}\right)\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +4\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}+\upkappa_{IB}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{BJ}^{(\infty)}\right)\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}+\upkappa_{IC}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{CJ}^{(\infty)}\right)\Big\}\\
&\ \ +\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{-4+\upsigma}).\end{split}

(6.23)

Consider now the symmetric matrix
K:=\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\right)_{I,J=1,\cdots,\mathfrak{D}}
,
whose eigenvalues are
-q_{I}^{(\infty)},\cdots,-q_{\mathfrak{D}}^{(\infty)}
.
Using that
for
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\text{tr}(K^{m})=\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\left[-q_{I}^{(\infty)}\right]^{m}
,
we rewrite the expression in braces on RHS (
6.23
) as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{AB}^{(\infty)}-\upkappa_{AJ}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{BI}^{(\infty)}\right)\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{AB}^{(\infty)}-\upkappa_{AJ}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{BI}^{(\infty)}\right)+4\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}+\upkappa_{IB}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{BJ}^{(\infty)}\right)\left(\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}+\upkappa_{IC}^{(\infty)}\upkappa_{CJ}^{(\infty)}\right)\\
&=2\left[\text{tr}(KK)\right]^{2}+4\text{tr}(KK)+8\text{tr}(KKK)+2\text{tr}(KKKK)\\
&=4\left\{\sum_{I=1}^{\mathfrak{D}}\left[(q_{I}^{(\infty)})^{2}-q_{I}^{(\infty)}\right]^{2}+\sum_{1\leq I<J\leq\mathfrak{D}}(q_{I}^{(\infty)})^{2}(q_{J}^{(\infty)})^{2}\right\}.\end{split}

(6.24)

Combining (
6.23
)–(
6.24
),
we arrive at the first equality stated in (
6.18
).
To prove the second equality stated in (
6.18
),
we simply use (
6.11b
)
to replace all factors of

\upkappa_{IJ}^{(\infty)}
on RHS (
6.23
)
with
-\widetilde{q}_{\underline{I}}\updelta_{\underline{I}J}

up to
\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon})
error terms
(which, in view of the factor of
t^{-4}
in front of the braces in (
6.23
),
leads to the error term
\mathcal{O}(\mathring{\upepsilon}t^{-4})

on RHS (
6.18
)).
This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

6.4
Proof of Theorems
6.1
and
6.6

We first prove Theorem
6.1
.
The conclusions regarding existence and norm estimates, generalized Kasner behavior, and blow up of curvature follow from Propositions
5.28
,
6.7
, and
6.8
,
and the estimate (
5.64
).
The
C^{2}
-inextendibility is a direct consequence of the curvature-blowup.

To prove Theorem
6.6
, we simply note that
the polarized
U(1)
-symmetric solutions satisfy the same estimates
as the solutions from Theorem
6.1
.
Hence, the same arguments used to prove Theorem
6.1

also yield Theorem
6.6
.
Finally, we note that the symmetry properties of polarized
U(1)
-symmetric solutions relative
to CMC-transported spatial coordinates stated in the conclusions of the theorem
are provided by Lemma
2.3
.

∎
